Title: A Liturgy, collected principally from the Book of Common Prayer, for the use of the First Episcopal Church in Boston; together with the Psalter, or Psalms of David
Creator: The First Episcopal Church in Boston (King's Chapel); the host catalogue names James Freeman as well as the church
Publisher: Boston: printed by Peter Edes, in State-Street
Publication Date: 1785
Rights: Public Domain (published 1785, more than 95 years ago; public domain by date under the project's rolling rule; the item carries no rights field, so the date rule alone applies)
Source: Internet Archive item liturgycollected00king (https://archive.org/details/liturgycollected00king), the item's own liturgycollected00king_djvu.txt full-text OCR derivative; contributing library per the item metadata: Boston Public Library; host metadata date: 1785. Fetched directly (curl/urllib, raw bytes) by the Library thread (Era VIII pass), 2026-10-23. sha256 of the raw fetched bytes: 3826fafb8daf119b27039549b49ac08224cffb92c6e637f54883e7a8e3fce835.
Provenance note: Header prepended by the Library thread; nothing else altered, no OCR error corrected, nothing retyped. Boston Public Library scan; usable OCR, the narrow liturgical columns giving short lines; long s as f.
Content note: The King's Chapel prayer book of 1785, with its preface on why the congregation, 'torn from that King and Church', revised the Book of Common Prayer: prayers addressed to God the Father through Christ, the Trinitarian forms removed, and the Psalter.

Bilhops,  Bifliops,  Priefts,  and  Deacons  of  that 
Church  are  obliged  to  take  an  oath  of  allegiance  and 
fupremacy,  at  the  time  of  their  confecration  or  or- 
dination, Being  torn  from  that  King  and  Church, 
the  Society  for  whofe  ufe  this  Liturgy  is  publilhed, 
think  themfelves  at  liberty,  and  well  juftified  even 
by  the  declarations  of  the  Church  of  England,  in 
making  fuch  alterations,  as  the  exigency  of  the  times 
and  occafions  hath  rendered  expedient,'’  and  in  ex-

themfelves  however  by  “ the  holy  fcriptures,which,” 
they  heartily  agree  with  the  Church  of  'England, 
contain  all  things  neceffary  to  falvation,”  and  that 
whatfoever  is  not  read  therein,  nor  can  be  proved

gland  was  compiled,  the  Committee  appointed  to  ex- 
ecute that  bufinefs  were  obliged  to  proceed  very  ten-

then  retained  which  have,  in  later  times,  given  great 
offence  to  many,  truly  pious,  Chriftians.

The  Liturgy,  contained  In  this  volume,  is  fuch,

In  compiling  this  Liturgy  great  affiftance  hath 
been  derived  from  the  judicious  corrections  of  the 
Reverend  Mr.  Lindfey,  who  hath  reformed  the 
Book  of  Common  Prayer  according  to  the  Plan  of 
the  truly  pious  and  juftly  celebrated  Dodtor  Samuel 
Clarke.  Several  of  Mr.  Lindfey’s  amendments 
are  adopted  entire.  The  alterations  which  are 
taken  from  him,  and  the  others  which*  are 
made,  excepting  the  prayers  for  Congrefs  and  the 
General  Court,  are  none  of  them  novelties  ; for 
they  have  been  propofed  and  juftified  by  fome  of  the 
firft  divines  of  the  Church  of  England.
